These areas leave a practical question: what should a successor do when it receives a useful handoff but the underlying document has changed? We did not find consistent evidence that Proofpress improves answer quality when documents are unchanged. [//]: # (ob:ba410001)
# Proofpress: An Artifact Provenance Protocol for Agent Handoffs

[//]: # (ob:ba410003)
**Working paper — Chenming Tang, Independent Researcher.** **ORCID:** [0009-0003-5666-0630](https://orcid.org/0009-0003-5666-0630). **Correspondence:** tangchenming0830@gmail.com. **Evidence status:** local evaluation through 12 August 2026; paper narrative refreshed 13 August 2026. The strongest result is a repeated, design-aligned mechanism finding, not a real-world product-effect estimate.

[//]: # (ob:ba410004)
> **Takeaway:** A handoff tells a successor agent what its predecessor knew. Proofpress addresses a different question: whether that inherited knowledge still belongs to the artifact the successor has now.

[//]: # (ob:ba410005)
## Abstract

[//]: # (ob:ba410006)
Prior research on agent memory studies how systems retain, update, and discard information, while agent-handoff benchmarks study whether a successor can continue earlier work. Provenance standards separately record how digital artifacts were produced and changed. These areas leave a practical question: what should a successor do when it receives a useful handoff but the underlying document has changed?

[//]: # (ob:ba410007)
We introduce **Proofpress, an artifact provenance protocol for agent handoffs**. Proofpress records which version of a document inherited claims and decisions refer to. Before continuing, a successor can check that relationship, reopen only the work affected by a change, or refuse a handoff whose history cannot be verified. The protocol complements readable context: the handoff explains prior work, while provenance establishes which artifact that work belongs to.

[//]: # (ob:ba410008)
We evaluated Proofpress using document-handoff tasks adapted from legal and scientific-evidence benchmarks, policy-change scenarios, and purpose-built documentation tasks. In the strongest preregistered comparison using `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, ordinary handoff reused work tied to an outdated document in 12/12 trials; Proofpress did so in 0/12, while both conditions continued correctly in all 12 unchanged-document trials. The same pattern was reproduced with GLM 5.2, Kimi K3, and Qwen 3.8 Max on the same task. Other studies showed that ordinary handoffs can recover when the contradiction is already visible, and that readable handoffs reduce repeated research. Across the wider program, however, we did not find consistent evidence that Proofpress improves answer quality when documents are unchanged.

[//]: # (ob:ba410009)
These results support a bounded conclusion: readable handoffs help agents continue prior work, while artifact provenance can prevent that work from being reused against the wrong document version. They do not establish a general increase in agent capability or a real-world average effect.

[//]: # (ob:ba41000a)
## 1. Introduction

[//]: # (ob:ba41000b)
Agent memory and handoff research has largely focused on retaining information, updating obsolete memories, reducing rediscovery, and completing work begun by another agent. [LongMemEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10813), [STALE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06527), and [Memora](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20006) test whether stored information remains useful as facts and preferences change; [Handoff Debt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02875) and [AgentAsk](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1294/) study continuity and error propagation when work passes between agents. This literature establishes that outdated information and incomplete handoffs are consequential, but it does not by itself identify which version of an independently changing document a transferred conclusion belongs to.

[//]: # (ob:ba41005f)
Agent handoff is already an explicit systems pattern rather than a hypothetical future workflow. OpenAI's [agent-building guide](https://openai.com/business/guides-and-resources/a-practical-guide-to-building-ai-agents/) defines decentralized handoff as transferring execution and the latest conversation state to another agent, while [GitHub coding agents](https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/copilot/concepts/agents/about-third-party-coding-agents) can work asynchronously on repository tasks and continue work through pull-request review. Empirical work also identifies the boundary as failure-prone: Handoff Debt finds substantial rediscovery costs when successor coding agents inherit incomplete context, and AgentAsk identifies data gaps, signal corruption, and referential drift in inter-agent messages. Importantly, Handoff Debt freezes the repository at takeover to isolate context transfer. That design establishes the handoff problem but intentionally removes the case studied here: the shared artifact changing after the predecessor's work was recorded.

[//]: # (ob:ba41000c)
Provenance research addresses a complementary problem. [W3C PROV](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-overview/) represents entities, activities, agents, and derivations; [in-toto](https://in-toto.io/), [TUF](https://theupdateframework.io/), [SLSA](https://slsa.dev/), and [Sigstore](https://www.sigstore.dev/) show how software consumers can verify identity, history, and policy before using an artifact. Recent agent systems such as [Portable Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11032), [MemLineage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14421), [StateAuditor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01619), and [ProvenanceGuard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18037) apply related ideas to memory transfer, ancestry, source support, or action gates. These systems provide direct precedent for verifiable lineage. The remaining gap studied here is narrower: a predecessor hands off claims and decisions, the external document changes independently, and the successor must decide whether the inherited work still applies.

[//]: # (ob:ba410060)
The risk created by mutable artifacts is likewise established outside agent-handoff research. The [W3C PROV model](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-constraints/) represents successive document versions as different entities connected by revision, generation, usage, and invalidation events. Production software workflows apply the same principle operationally: GitHub's [required-status-check guidance](https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-server@3.18/pull-requests/collaborating-with-pull-requests/collaborating-on-repositories-with-code-quality-features/troubleshooting-required-status-checks) can require a branch to be brought up to date and tested against the latest base before merge. These practices do not prove that agent handoffs routinely fail because of document changes. They establish the underlying systems hazard: work validated against one artifact state cannot automatically be assumed valid for a later state. This paper tests that intersection directly.

[//]: # (ob:ba41000d)
This paper introduces **Proofpress**, an artifact provenance protocol designed for that boundary. It distinguishes two problems that ordinary discussions of “agent memory” often combine:

[//]: # (ob:62bd04aa)
1. **Continuity:** missing context forces a successor to rediscover sources, decisions, and rationale.
2. **Version mismatch:** preserved context is reused after the underlying artifact has changed.

[//]: # (ob:ae1caf8d)
A strong memo can reduce the first problem. The second requires a relationship between the memo, the accepted artifact revision, and the revision now being used.

[//]: # (ob:ba41000e)
Proofpress records accepted artifact versions, claims, actors, and revision history in a verifiable carrier. For Markdown and static HTML, the reference implementation embeds this admitted history as an in-document portable capsule, so the native ledger can travel with the file without the original Git repository or agent session. Other file types use a format-aware or byte-bound evidence envelope rather than claiming an embedded semantic ledger. At handoff, the successor checks whether inherited claims still refer to the artifact currently in hand. This check can authorize continuation, reopen affected work, or stop reuse when the history cannot be verified. It does not decide whether a claim is true, whether a legal conclusion is correct, or whether a newer document is substantively better.

[//]: # (ob:ba41000f)
We make three contributions:

[//]: # (ob:3240f8d2)
1. We define an artifact provenance protocol for carrying claims, decisions, and revision history across an agent handoff.
2. We evaluate it against ordinary handoffs containing the same readable information, using tasks adapted from existing legal and scientific-evidence benchmarks together with policy and documentation tasks.
3. We report both the strongest result and its limits: provenance checks prevented reuse of work tied to an outdated document in the controlled studies, but did not consistently improve answer quality when documents were unchanged.

[//]: # (ob:ba410010)
## 2. Proofpress protocol

[//]: # (ob:ba410051)
### 2.1 Protocol overview

[//]: # (ob:ba410052)
Proofpress performs five operations: **record** the accepted document and its claims; **link** those claims to that document version; **transfer** the readable handoff and provenance record; **check** the document received by the successor; and **decide** whether to continue, revisit affected work, or refuse reuse. In the current implementation, Markdown and static HTML carry a hidden, declarative portable capsule inside the document; `inspect`, `import`, and `verify` can recover and check that admitted history on a fresh machine. DOCX, PDF, and arbitrary files use a separate evidence envelope with format-aware or byte-level verification. The protocol uses established identity and provenance primitives; its contribution is the handoff contract connecting those primitives to an agent's decision to reuse prior work.

[//]: # (ob:ac376353)
The Proofpress reference implementation is [open source under Apache-2.0](https://github.com/chenmingtang830/proofpress). Protocol behavior in this report was checked against repository commit `5ba7d8a25042a202b739eabbfd625096b5d9ab38`, retrieved on 13 August 2026. The study is packaged separately as a reviewable ARA release candidate so product source, experimental evidence, and research claims remain distinguishable.

[//]: # (ob:ba410011)
### 2.2 System model

[//]: # (ob:ba410012)
Let a predecessor produce document version `A_t`, handoff `H_t`, and accepted decisions `D_t`. A successor later receives document `A_t+1`. An ordinary handoff asks whether `H_t` contains enough information to continue. Proofpress asks an additional, mechanically checkable question: were the inherited decisions accepted for the document version now being used?

[//]: # (ob:ba410013)
```text
Originating agent                         Successor agent

artifact A_t ──► accepted decisions D_t ──► readable handoff H_t
      │                                              │
      └──── recorded version + provenance check ─────┤
                                                     ▼
                                             current artifact A_t+1
                                                     │
                              same accepted version ─┼──► continue
                              relevant change ───────┼──► revisit affected work
                              unverifiable history ──┴──► stop or refuse
```

[//]: # (ob:ba410014)
The purpose of the check is not to reject every change. A useful provenance protocol should preserve continuation when a revision is identical or irrelevant, reopen only affected claims when a change matters, and stop when the recorded history cannot support reuse.

[//]: # (ob:ba410015)
### 2.3 What Proofpress adds

[//]: # (ob:ba410016)
The ordinary handoff condition contains natural-language context: prior conclusions, rationale, caveats, open questions, and next steps. The Proofpress condition preserves that same content and adds a machine-checkable document version, artifact digest, claim-to-artifact relationship, and verification operation. This design treats the readable handoff as the continuity mechanism and the provenance check as the experimental intervention.

[//]: # (ob:ba410061)
The following schematic shows the information difference. It is a compact illustration of the experimental contract, not a verbatim model transcript:

[//]: # (ob:ba410062)
| Ordinary handoff | Proofpress-assisted handoff |
|---|---|
| “The predecessor reviewed the document, accepted conclusion C1, rejected route R1, and recommends next step N1.” | The same readable statement, plus: accepted artifact identity and digest; C1→artifact-version binding; actor and revision record; and a verification operation. |
| The successor can understand the conclusion but cannot independently determine whether it was accepted for the document now present. | The successor verifies the current artifact. A match permits continuation; a relevant mismatch scopes revalidation; unverifiable history stops reuse. |

[//]: # (ob:ba410017)
### 2.4 What Proofpress does not add

[//]: # (ob:ba410018)
Proofpress is not a language model, memory service, semantic conflict resolver, or truth oracle. A verified record establishes identity, integrity, and declared history under a specified policy. It does not establish that every recorded claim is correct or that a document remains authorized under information the receiver was never given.

[//]: # (ob:ba410019)
## 3. Evaluation design

[//]: # (ob:ba41001a)
### 3.1 Research questions

[//]: # (ob:ba41001b)
We organized the evaluation around four questions:

[//]: # (ob:8265ed30)
- **RQ1 — Continuity:** Does a readable handoff reduce repeated research?
- **RQ2 — Unchanged documents:** Does provenance improve the quality of work when the document has not changed?
- **RQ3 — Outdated documents:** Does checking document provenance prevent a successor from using work tied to an earlier version?
- **RQ4 — Selective recovery:** Can the successor ignore harmless edits while revisiting changes that affect prior claims?

[//]: # (ob:ba41001c)
### 3.2 Tasks and comparisons

[//]: # (ob:ba41001d)
We adapted materials from APEX-style legal-document work, HiEviDR scientific-evidence records, and policy revisions into predecessor-to-successor tasks. We also created small documentation tasks to test continuity and selective recovery. These are adaptations, not official benchmark submissions. The ordinary and Proofpress conditions received the same readable conclusions, rationale, and next steps. Only the Proofpress condition received a verifiable record linking that work to an accepted document version.

[//]: # (ob:a4691d62)
| Study family | Concrete successor task | Comparison | Question answered |
|---|---|---|---|
| Continuity | Continue three documentation jobs whose predecessor had already identified the relevant sources and next action | Artifacts only vs prose handoff vs structured handoff | Does any useful handoff prevent the successor from redoing source-finding work? |
| Controlled document-version check | Decide whether inherited work may be used after an APEX-derived legal document is either unchanged or replaced | Ordinary vs Proofpress; unchanged vs replaced document | Does provenance prevent reuse against the wrong version? |
| Scientific-evidence revision | Reassess an inherited synthesis after the underlying evidence record changes, with the contradiction either latent or visible | Ordinary vs Proofpress | When does provenance change the final decision rather than only the recovery path? |
| Policy revision | Apply inherited guidance after the governing policy text changes and remains readable to the successor | Ordinary vs Proofpress | Does explicit provenance cause grounded rechecking when both agents can read the new rule? |
| Selective recovery | Continue after harmless wording changes or revisit claims affected by documentation, evidence, and dependency changes | Harmless edit vs decision-relevant edit | Can the protocol revisit affected work without stopping on every change? |
| Clean-task studies | Complete legal, document-production, and research tasks when the received artifact has not changed | Ordinary vs Proofpress | Does provenance generally improve answer quality? |

[//]: # (ob:ba41001e)
### 3.3 Outcomes and evidence standards

[//]: # (ob:ba41001f)
We measured whether the successor repeated source discovery, continued correctly, revisited affected work, or reused a conclusion tied to an outdated document. **Repeated source discovery** means that the successor reopened or fetched a source the predecessor had already located solely because the inherited package did not preserve enough source context to continue; a deterministic scorer checked one predefined rediscovery event per continuity task. Artifact identity and protocol actions were scored deterministically. Semantic quality used blind model judging and human audit where required. Formal comparisons fixed model routes, prompts, task inputs, retry rules, and scoring contracts; provider fallback and cross-router retries were not allowed.

[//]: # (ob:ba410063)
The main quantitative tables combine studies with different receiver stacks. Their configurations are therefore reported explicitly rather than treated as one pooled model sample:

[//]: # (ob:ba410064)
| Result family | Receiver harness | Transport and provider | Exact receiver model | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Preregistered controlled document-version check | Restricted package-relative OpenRouter receiver with native verifier tool and macOS process confinement | OpenRouter; OpenAI only; fallback disabled | `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` (GPT-5.6 Luna; allowed resolved version `openai/gpt-5.6-luna-20260709`) | high |
| Three-model frozen-protocol follow-up | Same restricted package-relative receiver | OpenRouter; model-specific provider pinned; fallback disabled | `z-ai/glm-5.2`, `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, `qwen/qwen3.8-max` | low |
| Selective-recovery Track 2 | OpenCode in pure JSON mode with package-local tools | Vercel AI Gateway; provider pinned; fallback and cross-router retry disabled | `zai/glm-5.2`; `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` via DeepInfra | high |
| Continuity Track 1 | OpenCode in pure JSON mode | Vercel AI Gateway; DeepInfra pinned | `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` | high |

[//]: # (ob:ba410020)
We report repeated trials separately from task diversity. Repeating one task with several models shows that the observed behavior is not unique to one model, but it does not create several independent real-world cases. Engineering qualifications, invalid runs, and superseded analyses are disclosed in the appendices and are not pooled with the main comparisons.

[//]: # (ob:ba410021)
## 4. Results

[//]: # (ob:ba410022)
### 4.1 Main result: an artifact-version check prevented reuse against an outdated document

[//]: # (ob:ba410023)
The strongest study isolated one decision: whether a successor should continue using inherited work after the underlying document was replaced. In the preregistered GPT-5.6 Luna comparison, run at high effort through the restricted package-relative harness described above, ordinary handoff continued in all 12 outdated-document trials; Proofpress continued in none. Both conditions continued correctly in all 12 unchanged-document trials.

[//]: # (ob:ba410024)
The frozen comparison was then repeated with GLM 5.2, Kimi K3, and Qwen 3.8 Max. Every model showed the same pattern. These are repeated model runs on one controlled task, so the combined counts describe reliability within that task rather than performance over independent documents.

[//]: # (ob:14648c7e)
| Model study | Condition | Unchanged document: correct continue | Outdated document: incorrect continue |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| GPT-5.6 Luna (`openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, high) | Ordinary handoff | 12/12 | 12/12 |
| GPT-5.6 Luna (`openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, high) | Proofpress | 12/12 | 0/12 |
| GLM 5.2 (`z-ai/glm-5.2`, low) | Ordinary handoff | 12/12 | 12/12 |
| GLM 5.2 (`z-ai/glm-5.2`, low) | Proofpress | 12/12 | 0/12 |
| Kimi K3 (`moonshotai/kimi-k3`, low) | Ordinary handoff | 12/12 | 12/12 |
| Kimi K3 (`moonshotai/kimi-k3`, low) | Proofpress | 12/12 | 0/12 |
| Qwen 3.8 Max (`qwen/qwen3.8-max`, low) | Ordinary handoff | 12/12 | 12/12 |
| Qwen 3.8 Max (`qwen/qwen3.8-max`, low) | Proofpress | 12/12 | 0/12 |

[//]: # (ob:ba410025)
### 4.2 The benefit depends on whether the problem is otherwise visible

[//]: # (ob:ba410026)
The controlled result was strongest when the inherited conclusion still looked plausible and only the document-version check exposed the problem. A HiEviDR-derived task showed the same separation under that condition: Proofpress avoided incorrect continuation in 9/9 trials, while ordinary handoff continued incorrectly in 9/9.

[//]: # (ob:ba410027)
When the current evidence directly contradicted the inherited conclusion, both conditions avoided the wrong final answer. Proofpress consistently reopened and grounded the earlier work; ordinary handoff sometimes explicitly rechecked it and sometimes corrected itself from the visible evidence. Policy tasks produced the same boundary: provenance changed how recovery occurred, but not the final safety rate.

[//]: # (ob:f6073d74)
| Task condition | Ordinary handoff | Proofpress | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Scientific-evidence change, mismatch not obvious | 9/9 incorrect continues | 0/9 incorrect continues | Provenance changed the final decision |
| Scientific-evidence change, contradiction visible | 0/9 incorrect outcomes; 5/9 explicit recoveries | 0/9 incorrect outcomes; 9/9 explicit recoveries | Both were safe; provenance changed the recovery path |
| Policy change, current rule visible | 0/18 incorrect outcomes; 0/18 provenance-grounded recoveries | 0/18 incorrect outcomes; 18/18 provenance-grounded recoveries | Both were safe; provenance made rechecking explicit |

[//]: # (ob:ba410028)
### 4.3 Proofpress supported selective recovery rather than blanket rejection

[//]: # (ob:ba410029)
Across six documentation and evidence-update cases run through OpenCode at high effort with `zai/glm-5.2` and `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`, Proofpress continued on all 6 harmless edits and revisited the affected work on all 6 decision-relevant edits. Ordinary handoff also continued on all harmless edits, but continued without targeted rechecking in 4 of the 6 relevant-edit responses.

[//]: # (ob:ba41002a)
This matters because a useful provenance protocol should not halt work after every byte-level edit. The intended behavior is narrower: preserve continuation when a change is irrelevant and reopen only the claims whose basis may have changed.

[//]: # (ob:ba41002b)
| Change to the document | Ordinary handoff | Proofpress |
|---|---:|---:|
| Harmless edit | Continued 6/6 | Continued 6/6 |
| Edit affecting an inherited claim | Targeted recheck 2/6; continued 4/6 | Targeted recheck 6/6 |

[//]: # (ob:ba41002c)
### 4.4 A readable handoff reduced repeated research

[//]: # (ob:ba41002d)
Three documentation tasks used OpenCode at high effort with `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` and compared artifacts alone, a prose handoff, and a structured handoff. With artifacts alone, the successor reopened a source the predecessor had already found in 3/3 tasks. With either prose or structured handoff, it did so in 0/3. This supports the value of a competent handoff, but does not show that structured provenance is better than well-written prose when the document is unchanged.

[//]: # (ob:ba41002e)
| Information given to successor | Repeated source discovery |
|---|---:|
| Artifacts only | 3/3 |
| Prose handoff | 0/3 |
| Structured handoff | 0/3 |

[//]: # (ob:ba41002f)
### 4.5 We did not find a general answer-quality improvement

[//]: # (ob:ba410030)
Across legal, document-production, restricted-access, policy, and scientific-evidence studies using unchanged inputs, results were usually tied, mixed, or limited by the underlying task. Ordinary and Proofpress handoffs tied at 14/18 on the APEX Law rubric and 15/21 in its blind audit; the Restricted-Access Lease audit tied at 13/21. In another document workflow, every condition failed the clean completion criterion because the underlying document task failed.

[//]: # (ob:ba410031)
These null and failed studies bound the claim. Provenance cannot replace missing evidence, repair an underspecified task, or improve document execution by itself. The current evidence does not support presenting Proofpress as a general agent-quality booster.

[//]: # (ob:ba410032)
One additional memory study was sensitive to the semantic judge: the same frozen answers favored Proofpress under one scorer and favored the comparison under two others. We therefore treat that study as evidence about measurement sensitivity, not product efficacy; full details appear in the appendix.

[//]: # (ob:ba410059)
### 4.6 Overhead varied by study, model, and telemetry coverage

[//]: # (ob:ba41005a)
The preregistered GPT-5.6 Luna study and the three-model controlled follow-up recorded token usage, wall-clock time, and provider-reported model cost for every formal-valid run. Because Luna used high effort while the follow-up models used low effort, and because token behavior, latency, and pricing differ by model, the table reports every study-model and condition separately. Only the three low-effort follow-up models are pooled.

[//]: # (ob:ba41005b)
| Study model and effort | Condition | Valid runs | Mean tokens | Mean wall time | Mean model cost |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| GPT-5.6 Luna (high) | Ordinary handoff | 24 | 55,244 | 14.6 s | $0.0013 |
| GPT-5.6 Luna (high) | Proofpress | 24 | 164,863 | 17.7 s | $0.0041 |
| GLM 5.2 (low) | Ordinary handoff | 24 | 5,829 | 27.3 s | $0.0060 |
| GLM 5.2 (low) | Proofpress | 24 | 17,544 | 37.1 s | $0.0147 |
| Kimi K3 (low) | Ordinary handoff | 24 | 5,699 | 38.1 s | $0.0136 |
| Kimi K3 (low) | Proofpress | 24 | 16,718 | 44.7 s | $0.0378 |
| Qwen 3.8 Max (low) | Ordinary handoff | 24 | 7,614 | 24.0 s | $0.0143 |
| Qwen 3.8 Max (low) | Proofpress | 24 | 21,247 | 37.3 s | $0.0286 |
| **Three-model follow-up pooled (low)** | **Ordinary handoff** | **72** | **6,381** | **29.8 s** | **$0.0113** |
| **Three-model follow-up pooled (low)** | **Proofpress** | **72** | **18,503** | **39.7 s** | **$0.0270** |

[//]: # (ob:ba41005c)
These are end-to-end harness comparisons, not isolated benchmarks of the deterministic protocol. The Proofpress condition included a larger carrier, verifier tool calls, and additional agent turns. In the low-effort follow-up, its token ratio ranged from 2.79× for Qwen to 3.01× for GLM, its wall-time ratio from 1.17× for Kimi to 1.55× for Qwen, and its model-cost ratio from 2.00× for Qwen to 2.78× for Kimi. In the separate high-effort Luna study, the corresponding ratios were 2.98×, 1.21×, and 3.15×. These differences cannot be attributed to hashing or verification alone.

[//]: # (ob:5e6a26c0)
The high-effort DeepSeek selective-recovery study used a different task and the OpenCode–AI Gateway harness, so it is not directly pooled with the controlled document-version runs. OpenCode's response summary reported zero cost, but the underlying AI Gateway request ledger retained tokens, request duration, and both market and total Gateway cost. We therefore reconstructed condition-level means from the ordered request ledger rather than treating the response-summary zero as free inference.

[//]: # (ob:aa1f2aee)
| DeepSeek Track 2 condition | Valid responses | Gateway requests | Mean Gateway tokens | Mean cumulative Gateway request time | Mean market model cost |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| V0 ordinary handoff | 6 | 39 | 42,895 | 42.0 s | $0.00185 |
| V1 Proofpress | 6 | 43 | 47,631 | 46.5 s | $0.00227 |

[//]: # (ob:57827d0a)
For DeepSeek, V1 used modestly more Gateway-billed tokens, cumulative Gateway request time, and market model cost than V0 in this small, different-task slice. The local Gateway ledger contains 82 successful calls and $0.024748 in market model cost; the tag-filtered Gateway aggregate report returned 81 calls and $0.024303, so the one-call reconciliation difference is disclosed and the condition split uses the persisted request-level ledger.

[//]: # (ob:3170fcc3)
This receive-time work should be interpreted against what the protocol is designed to prevent: an inherited conclusion being applied to the wrong artifact and propagating into later drafting, review, or decision steps. In the controlled outdated-document studies, the extra verification path accompanied the elimination of incorrect continuation in the tested Proofpress cells. The evaluation did not, however, monetize downstream rework, human audit, delayed discovery, or propagated-error costs. It therefore cannot establish that the added inference cost produces net savings; measuring that tradeoff is a production-evaluation question.

[//]: # (ob:ba410033)
## 5. Discussion

[//]: # (ob:ba410034)
### 5.1 The central result

[//]: # (ob:ba410035)
The experiments separate two functions that are often collapsed into “memory.” Natural-language handoff carries meaning: conclusions, rationale, caveats, and next steps. Artifact provenance carries applicability: which document those conclusions and decisions were made for. Proofpress's distinctive contribution is the second relationship, not additional prose.

[//]: # (ob:ba410036)
This distinction explains both the positive and null results. When a document is unchanged and the handoff is good, provenance has little reason to improve the final answer. When the new document obviously contradicts the old conclusion, a capable model may correct itself without a provenance record. The clearest benefit appears when inherited work remains plausible even though the underlying document has changed.

[//]: # (ob:ba410037)
### 5.2 Product implication

[//]: # (ob:ba410038)
The supported product claim is narrow but useful: **Proofpress gives successor agents a portable, independently checkable provenance record for inherited work. In the tested tasks, it prevented reuse against the wrong document version and prompted consistent rechecking of decision-relevant changes without blocking harmless edits.**

[//]: # (ob:ba410039)
The relevant comparison is therefore not “Proofpress versus memory.” A practical system needs both. The handoff explains prior work; Proofpress verifies which artifact that work belongs to.

[//]: # (ob:ba41003a)
### 5.3 Why the negative results matter

[//]: # (ob:ba41003b)
The lack of a broad quality advantage prevents the strongest controlled result from becoming a vague capability claim. Provenance does not make weak research complete, repair a broken document workflow, or turn a recorded assertion into truth. Its demonstrated value is concentrated at a handoff boundary: continue, revisit, or refuse inherited work.

[//]: # (ob:ba41003c)
### 5.4 Measurement sensitivity

[//]: # (ob:ba41003d)
The STALE/CUPMem study illustrates why semantic outcomes require caution. Its preregistered Nano scorer gave Proofpress a paired +8.33 percentage-point advantage over an information-matched flat record, with 95% CI [+1.25, +15.42]. Two Gemini rescoring passes on the same frozen answers gave −1.67 and −2.08 points. Much of the disagreement concerned whether a source-grounded refusal to answer counted as correct. We therefore treat this result as evidence that scoring policy can dominate the measured effect, not as scorer-independent product evidence.

[//]: # (ob:ba41003e)
## 6. Related work

[//]: # (ob:ba41003f)
### 6.1 Agent memory and handoff

[//]: # (ob:0d91390e)
Long-context and evolving-memory evaluations such as [LongMemEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10813), [STALE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06527), and [Memora](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20006) show that agents must update, invalidate, or abstain on obsolete information. [Handoff Debt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02875) measures successor rediscovery under coding-agent handoffs, while [AgentAsk](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1294/) examines error propagation at handoff boundaries. These works establish stale-state and handoff failures, but they do not isolate whether inherited knowledge remains bound to an independently mutable external artifact.

[//]: # (ob:ba410053)
### 6.2 Portable memory and provenance-aware agents

[//]: # (ob:ba410054)
[Portable Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11032) and [MemLineage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14421) introduce cryptographically verifiable or lineage-aware cross-agent memory. [StateAuditor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01619) and [ProvenanceGuard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18037) combine deterministic provenance checks with repair, re-verification, or action gates. They are direct precedents for verifier-aware agent behavior. Proofpress differs in the evaluated object: accepted work is transferred between agents, the external document can change independently, and the measured decision is whether earlier work is reused against that changed document.

[//]: # (ob:ba410040)
### 6.3 Provenance and verification before use

[//]: # (ob:5e2b5905)
Provenance standards and software supply-chain systems—including [W3C PROV](https://www.w3.org/TR/prov-overview/), [in-toto](https://in-toto.io/), [TUF](https://theupdateframework.io/), [SLSA](https://slsa.dev/), and [Sigstore](https://www.sigstore.dev/)—already establish entity lineage, immutable identity, attestations, rollback protection, and verification before use. Proofpress does not claim to invent these primitives. It applies that pattern to agent-produced knowledge artifacts and makes the successor's decision to reuse prior work explicit.

[//]: # (ob:ba410055)
### 6.4 Scoped research gap

[//]: # (ob:ba410056)
The literature therefore supplies each broad ingredient separately. Within our structured primary-source scoping review, we did not identify an evaluation that gives two successors the same readable handoff, changes an external artifact after that handoff was recorded, lets only one successor verify the artifact version, and measures whether outdated work is reused. That is the scoped gap addressed here. It is not a claim that Proofpress invented provenance, content addressing, stale-state evaluation, or agent handoff. The complete search method, comparison tables, and non-novelty boundary are maintained in `logic/related_work.md`.

[//]: # (ob:ba410041)
## 7. Limitations

[//]: # (ob:ba410042)
First, the strongest 100% separation is a **mechanism test aligned with Proofpress's design**: the task deliberately creates the document-version mismatch that Proofpress is built to detect, and only the Proofpress condition receives the check that reveals it. The result shows that the version-binding and admission path worked reliably under those controlled conditions. It does not show how often the condition occurs in practice, how well the protocol handles unanticipated forms of change, or whether the effect generalizes to independently authored workflows.

[//]: # (ob:ba41005d)
Second, repeated trials and cross-model replication do not create independent task diversity. Third, most tasks were constructed or adapted locally; they do not establish official APEX or HiEviDR benchmark performance. Fourth, the evaluated Proofpress path used substantially more tokens, wall time, and model spend, while the experiment did not isolate deterministic verification cost from carrier size, tool interaction, and recovery turns. Fifth, unnecessary stops, user trust, and long-term organizational benefit remain undermeasured. Sixth, Proofpress verifies declared artifact history, not source truth, legal correctness, or authorization outside the supplied policy.

[//]: # (ob:ba410043)
The next meaningful test is independently authored or naturally occurring document revision: harmless, ambiguous, and material changes; incomplete or conflicting history; receiver environments operated by another team; and explicit measurement of unnecessary stops and recovery cost. More calls on the existing controlled task would measure receiver consistency without addressing real-world coverage.

[//]: # (ob:ba410044)
## 8. Conclusion

[//]: # (ob:ba410045)
Agent handoff is not one problem. A successor needs enough context to avoid repeating the predecessor's work, and it needs a way to know which artifact that context belongs to. Our local experiments show that good handoffs address the first need. They do not show that provenance broadly improves work on unchanged documents. They do show, consistently within the tested tasks, that artifact-version checks can prevent reuse against the wrong document and prompt targeted review when a relevant change occurs.

[//]: # (ob:ba410046)
The clearest way to understand Proofpress is therefore not as “better memory,” but as an artifact provenance protocol for portable agent work.

[//]: # (ob:ba410047)
## Appendix A — experiment and evidence map

[//]: # (ob:ba410048)
The complete experiment registry, denominators, validity boundaries, and result pointers are maintained inside this ARA in [`logic/experiments.md`](logic/experiments.md). Reader-facing aggregates are in [`evidence/tables/derived_main_results.md`](evidence/tables/derived_main_results.md), and machine-readable summaries are indexed by [`evidence/README.md`](evidence/README.md). The main text intentionally presents only the task-level comparisons needed to evaluate the claims.

[//]: # (ob:ba410049)
| Evidence family | Role in this paper | Main-body use |
|---|---|---|
| Knowledge Handoff Tracks 1–2 | Continuity and selective version-drift mechanism | RQ1 and RQ4 |
| APEX-direct, Luna R1, three-model follow-up | Clean/stale admission mechanism and model robustness | Primary RQ3 evidence |
| HiEviDR-derived and Policy R1 | Visible-evidence and recovery-path boundary | Qualification of RQ3 |
| Sidecar, Harvey, APEX Law, Restricted Lease, Artifact Travel | Feasibility, negative controls, and clean-quality boundary | RQ2 synthesis |
| MemConflict and STALE/CUPMem | Historical stale-authority signal and judge sensitivity | Measurement boundary |

[//]: # (ob:ba41004a)
## Appendix B — invalid, excluded, and superseded paths

[//]: # (ob:ba41004b)
Invalid runs are not silently folded into the narrative. Major categories include transport failures; missing output contracts; a synthetic capsule exposed by the APEX-direct v1 harness; mixed transport in Artifact Travel v2; missing reviewer events in v4; a disabled verifier in v5; scorer disagreement in STALE; and repaired missing-checkpoint or timeout cells in Knowledge Handoff. Path-specific records and replacement rules remain in the ARA experiment registry and evidence index rather than the main narrative.

[//]: # (ob:ba41004c)
## Appendix C — reproducibility and claim boundaries

[//]: # (ob:ba41004d)
Machine-readable summaries, immutable reports, hashes, routing records, and Proofpress claim files are stored under `evidence/` and `../evidence/`. Formal comparisons require exact model and provider resolution, no fallback, valid output contracts, complete required artifacts, and parity across all inputs except the declared treatment.

[//]: # (ob:ba41005e)
The Proofpress implementation is publicly available at [github.com/chenmingtang830/proofpress](https://github.com/chenmingtang830/proofpress). This ARA is packaged as study release candidate `rc1`: local machine paths have been removed, aggregate evidence is included, intentionally non-redistributed raw records are labeled, and the paper, study-authored figures, and aggregate data are licensed under CC BY 4.0. A fixed public repository commit and tag remain necessary before citation as a stable release.

[//]: # (ob:ba41004e)
The paper supports three claims: handoff can reduce visible rediscovery; generic clean-quality improvement has not been established; and version binding governs admission in the tested drift fixtures. It does not support a pooled product-effect estimate, an official external benchmark score, a truth-verification claim, or a claim that Proofpress replaces shared memory.

[//]: # (ob:ba41004f)
